\displaystyle=\delta^{5}\int_{\partial B}\Bigg|\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{\delta}}\int_{B}\Phi(\xi,\tilde{t};\eta,\tilde{\tau})\hat{\psi}(\eta,\tilde{\tau})d\eta d\tilde{\tau}\Bigg|^{2}d\sigma_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\delta^{5}\Big\|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\partial B)}^{2}.

(3.59)

Based on these two expressions, we can calculate the following norms

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\mathbb{R};H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial\Omega)\Big)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{\partial\Omega}\dfrac{\Big\|\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big](x,\cdot)-\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big](y,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}}{|x-y|^{2}}d\sigma_{\mathrm{x}}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}\int_{\partial B}\int_{\partial B}\dfrac{\alpha\delta^{6}\Big\|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big](\xi,\cdot)-\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big](\eta,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}}{\delta^{2}|\xi-\eta|^{2}}d\sigma_{\xi}d\sigma_{\eta}

\displaystyle=\alpha\delta^{6}\Big|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\mathbb{R};H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\partial B)\Big)}^{2}

(3.60)

and

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)\Big)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{-\infty}^{T}\dfrac{\Big\|\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big](\cdot,t)-\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big](\cdot,\tau)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\partial\Omega)}^{2}}{|t-\tau|^{\frac{3}{2}}}d\mathrm{t}d\tau

\displaystyle=\alpha^{2}\delta^{4}\int_{-\infty}^{T_{\delta}}\int_{-\infty}^{T_{\delta}}\dfrac{\delta^{5}\Big\|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big](\xi,\cdot)-\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big](\eta,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\partial B)}^{2}}{\alpha^{\frac{3}{2}}\delta^{3}|\tilde{t}-\tilde{\tau}|^{\frac{3}{2}}}d\tilde{t}d\tilde{\tau}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{6}\Big|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\Psi}\Big]\Big|_{H^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\mathbb{R};\mathrm{L}^{2}(\partial B)\Big)}^{2}

(3.61)

Hence, considering the norm-calculation (
3.4
), (
3.4
) and (
3.4
), from the norm definition (
3.1
), noticing the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\alpha\delta^{7}}\leq\alpha\delta^{6}\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{6}}
, we get our desired inequality

\displaystyle\alpha\delta^{7}\Big\|\noindent\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}\leq\Big\|\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{6}\Big\|\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}

(3.62)

Hence it completes the proof.

Lemma 3.13

Suppose
0<\delta\leq 1
,
\Omega:=\delta B+\mathrm{z}
and
t:=\alpha\delta^{2}\tilde{t}
. Then for
\psi\in L_{2}\Big(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
we have the following

\displaystyle\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}}\Big[\psi\Big](\mathrm{x},t)=\delta\Big(\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\xi}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{B\times\mathbb{R}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big)^{\vee}

(3.63)

and

\displaystyle\alpha^{\frac{3}{4}}\delta^{3}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\xi}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\leq\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{{}^{2}}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\xi}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(3.64)

Proof.
Before proceeding to prove the first identity, we recall that on the boundary
(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}
i.e.
\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathrm{u}=\partial_{\nu}u,
with
\nu
being the outward unit normal vector to
\partial\Omega.

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu_{\mathrm{x}}}\mathcal{V}_{\Omega\times\mathbb{R}}\Big[\psi\Big](\mathrm{x},t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial_{\nu_{\mathrm{x}}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\psi(y,\tau)d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{\Omega}-\dfrac{\alpha(x-y)\cdot\nu_{y}}{2(t-\tau)}\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\psi(y,\tau)d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{\delta}}\int_{B}-\dfrac{\alpha\delta(\xi-\eta)\cdot\nu_{\eta}}{2\alpha\delta^{2}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{\tau})}\cdot\dfrac{\alpha}{4\pi\alpha\delta^{2}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{\tau})}e^{-\dfrac{\alpha\delta^{2}|\xi-\eta|^{2}}{4\alpha\delta^{2}(\tilde{t}-\tilde{\tau})}}\hat{\psi}(\eta,\tilde{\tau})\alpha\delta^{4}d\eta d\tilde{\tau}

\displaystyle=\delta\partial_{\nu_{\xi}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{\delta}}\int_{B}\Phi(\xi,\tilde{t};\eta,\tilde{\tau})\hat{\psi}(\eta,\tilde{\tau})d\eta d\tilde{\tau}

\displaystyle=\delta\partial_{\nu_{\xi}}\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{B\times\mathbb{R}}\Big[\hat{\Psi}\Big](\hat{x},\hat{\tau}),

(3.65)

where
\partial_{\nu_{\xi}}\overline{\mathcal{V}}
is related to the Newtonian heat operator with
\alpha=1
and corresponding to the source
\psi
. Hence the first identity is proved.

Now, to get the desired inequality, we need to scale the product
\Big\langle\varphi,\partial_{\nu_{\mathrm{x}}}\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\rangle_{\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}}
.

\displaystyle\Big\langle\varphi,\partial_{\nu_{\mathrm{x}}}\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\rangle_{\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{\partial\Omega}\varphi(\mathrm{x},t)\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial_{\nu_{\mathrm{x}}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\psi(y,\tau)d\mathrm{y}d\tau d\mathrm{y}d\tau d\sigma_{\mathrm{x}}d\mathrm{t}

\displaystyle=\alpha\delta^{4}\int_{-\infty}^{T_{\delta}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\varphi(\xi,\tilde{t})\partial_{\nu_{\xi}}\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{T_{\delta}}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\xi,\tilde{t};\eta,\tilde{\tau})\hat{\psi}(\xi,\tilde{\tau})d\eta d\tilde{\tau}d\sigma_{\xi}d\tilde{t}

\displaystyle=\alpha\delta^{4}\Big\langle\hat{\varphi},\partial_{\nu_{\xi}}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\rangle_{\partial B\times\mathbb{R}}.

(3.66)

Hence, from the definition of norm definition (
3.40
), we get our desired second inequality,

\displaystyle\alpha^{\frac{3}{4}}\delta^{3}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\xi}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\leq\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\xi}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(3.67)

Thus it completes the proof.

Lemma 3.14

For
\psi\in\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)
we have the following

\displaystyle\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{2}\Big\|\overline{\mathbb{I}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)}\leq\Big\|\mathbb{I}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)}\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta\Big\|\overline{\mathbb{I}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)}.

(3.68)

Proof.
We define the initial heat potential for
\psi\in\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)
as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Big[\psi\Big](\mathrm{x},t)=\int_{\Omega}\Phi(x,t;y)\psi(y)d\mathrm{y}.

We also note that for
\psi\in\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)
, we have the following two estimates

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{(\Omega)}\Big[\psi\Big](\mathrm{x},t)=\Big(\overline{\mathbb{I}}_{(B)}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big)^{\vee},

(3.69)

and

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathrm{x}}\mathbb{I}_{(\Omega)}\Big[\psi\Big](\mathrm{x},t)=\frac{1}{\delta}\Big(\nabla_{\xi}\overline{\mathbb{I}}_{(B)}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big)^{\vee}.

(3.70)

From the definition of initial heat potential we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{(\Omega)}\Big[\psi\Big](\mathrm{x},t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\Phi(x,t;y)\psi(y)d\mathrm{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\alpha}{4\pi t}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\frac{\alpha|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{2}}{4t}\Big)\psi(y)d\mathrm{y}

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}\int_{B}\frac{\alpha}{4\pi\alpha\delta^{2}\tilde{t}}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\frac{|\xi-\eta|^{2}}{4\tilde{t}}\Big)\psi(\delta\eta+\mathrm{z})d\eta

\displaystyle=\int_{B}\frac{1}{4\pi\tilde{t}}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\frac{|\xi-\eta|^{2}}{4\tilde{t}}\Big)\hat{\psi}(\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle=\Big(\overline{\mathbb{I}}_{(B)}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big)^{\vee}.

Hence, it completes the first inequality (
3.69
). Next, we provide the proof of the second inequality (
3.70
). So,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathrm{x}}\mathbb{I}_{(\Omega)}\Big[\psi\Big](\mathrm{x},t)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\mathrm{x}}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(x,t;y)\psi(y)d\mathrm{y}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\frac{\alpha^{2}|x-y|}{8\pi t^{2}}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\frac{\alpha|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{2}}{4t}\Big)\psi(y)d\mathrm{y}

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}\int_{B}\frac{\alpha^{2}\delta|\xi-\eta|}{8\pi\alpha^{2}\delta^{4}\tilde{t}}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\frac{|\xi-\eta|^{2}}{4\tilde{t}}\Big)\psi(\delta\eta+\mathrm{z})d\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{B}\frac{1}{8\pi\tilde{t}}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\frac{|\xi-\eta|^{2}}{4\tilde{t}}\Big)\hat{\psi}(\eta)d\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\Big(\nabla_{\xi}\overline{\mathbb{I}}_{(B)}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big)^{\vee},

which leads to (
3.70
).
Consequently, from the above two estimates we deduce

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{I}_{(\Omega)}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\Bigg|\int_{\Omega}\Phi(x,t;y)\psi(y)d\mathrm{y}\Bigg|^{2}d\mathrm{x}dt

\displaystyle=\alpha\delta^{4}\int_{0}^{T_{\delta}}\int_{B}\Bigg|\int_{B}\hat{\Phi}(\xi,\tilde{t};\eta)\hat{\psi}(\eta)d\eta\Bigg|^{2}d\xi d\tilde{t}

\displaystyle=\alpha\delta^{4}\Big\|\mathbb{I}_{(B)}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B\times\mathbb{R_{+}})}^{2}.

(3.71)

In a similar manner, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big\|\nabla_{\mathrm{x}}\mathbb{I}_{(\Omega)}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R_{+}})}^{2}=\alpha\delta^{2}\Big\|\nabla_{\xi}\mathbb{I}_{(B)}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B\times\mathbb{R_{+}})}^{2},

(3.72)

and

\displaystyle\Big\|\partial_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{I}_{(\Omega)}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times\mathbb{R_{+}})}^{2}=\alpha\delta^{2}\Big\|\partial_{\tilde{t}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{I}_{(B)}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(B\times\mathbb{R_{+}})}^{2}.

(3.73)

Consequently, from the above three estimates,
H^{1,\frac{1}{2}}
-norm definition and noticing the fact that
\alpha\delta^{4}\leq\alpha\delta^{2}
we obtain the desired inequality

\displaystyle\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{2}\Big\|\overline{\mathbb{I}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R_{+}}\Big)}\leq\Big\|\mathbb{I}\Big[\psi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)}\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta\Big\|\overline{\mathbb{I}}\Big[\hat{\psi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)}.

(3.74)

4
Proof of Theorem
1.3

In this section, we show the asymptotic analysis of the solution to (
1.8
) as
\delta\to 0
when a plasmonic nanoparticle occupy a bounded domain
\Omega=\mathrm{z}+\delta\mathrm{B}.

4.1
A Priori Estimates of the Heat Potential

We start by stating the following Proposition.

Proposition 4.0.1

We have the following a priori estimate for the solution to the boundary integral equations (
2.7
)

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{2}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.1)

and

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big(\alpha^{\frac{1}{4}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta+\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big)\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.2)

Proof.
We start with the following equation for
(\mathrm{x},t)\in\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{x},t)=-\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}+\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big)^{-1}\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\mathbb{A}^{\textbf{ext}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big](\mathrm{x},t)+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}+\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big)^{-1}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T_{0}})}\Big](\mathrm{x},t)

(4.3)

From Lemma
3.1
and taking
\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big((\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
-norm on the both sides of the equation (
4.3
) , we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big\|\Big[\frac{1}{2}I_{d}+\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big]^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R})\Big)}\cdot\Big\|\mathcal{S}_{\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}{\Big(\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big((\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R})\Big),\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big((\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R)}\Big)}\Big)}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\|\mathbb{A}^{\textbf{ext}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big\|\Big[\frac{1}{2}I_{d}+\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big]^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R})\Big)}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T_{0}})}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big((\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(4.4)

Furthermore, from Lemma
3.7
, Lemma
3.11
and Lemma
3.1
, i.e. the continuity of the operator
\overline{\mathcal{S}}:\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\to\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\varphi\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{2}\Big\|\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\hat{\varphi}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{2}\Big\|\hat{\varphi}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\varphi\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(4.5)

Similar as in the proof of the third assertion of the Lemma
3.10
, we conclude

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{S}_{\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}{\Big(\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big),\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big)}\leq\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\partial B\times\mathbb{R}}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}{\Big(\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big),\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big)}.

(4.6)

Next, we use Lemma
3.7
, Lemma
3.12
, Corollary
3.4.1
, i.e. the continuity of the operator
\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\overline{\mathcal{V}}:\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}\times\mathbb{R})\to\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
, and due to the fact that
|\mathrm{E}|^{2}
is independent of the time
t\in(0,T_{0})
, we deduce

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T_{0}})}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{3}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq C\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{3}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq CT_{0}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{2}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.7)

As we move on to the next step, it should be noted that the continuity of the hyper-singular operator
\mathbb{H}:\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
is determined by the mapping property of the single and double layer operator, as well as the definition of Calderón projector. Now, if we recall the identity of the Calderón projector
\mathcal{C}
, we note that for
\varphi\in\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}\mathbb{H}\Big[\varphi\Big]=(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K})(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}+\mathcal{K})\Big[\varphi\Big].

(4.8)

Therefore, if we consider the appropriate norm, we deduce the following

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{H}[\varphi]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\mathcal{S}^{-1}_{\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}{\Big(\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big),\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big)}\cdot\Big\|\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R})\Big)}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\|\Big[\frac{1}{2}I_{d}+\mathcal{K}\Big](\varphi)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(4.10)

Now, Lemma
3.10
and the non-scalability of the double layer heat operator allows us to compute

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{H}[\varphi]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\lesssim\delta^{-1}\Big\|\varphi\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(4.11)

Additionally, the Steklov-Poincaré operator
\mathbb{A}^{\textbf{ext}}:\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
for the heat equation is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{\textbf{ext}}:=\mathbb{H}+\Big(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}^{*}\Big)\mathcal{S}^{-1}\Big(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}\Big).

(4.12)

Accordingly, considering the bounds of the operators
\mathcal{S}^{-1},\mathbb{H},\mathcal{K}\ \text{and}\ \mathcal{K}^{*},
we can also conclude that the operator
\mathbb{A}^{\textbf{ext}}
is bounded. Next, we can see from the expression (
4.12
) that scaling depends on the operators
\mathcal{S}^{-1}
and
\mathbb{H}
respectively, since the two other operators do not scale.
Therefore, we obtain the following from equation (
4.11
)

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{A}^{\textbf{ext}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\lesssim\delta^{-1}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(4.13)

We are now in a position to provide a priori estimate of
\gamma_{0}^{\text{int}}\mathrm{U}_{i}
. We consider (
4.1
), (
4.1
), (
4.11
), (
4.13
), Lemma
3.2
and plugging these estimates into (
4.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{-1}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{2}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)},

(4.14)

and then

\displaystyle\Big(1-\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{-1}\Big)\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{2}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.15)

Hence, due to the assumptions in (
1.17
), we have

\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{-1}<1,

(4.16)

for
\delta<<1
, which implies that

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{2}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.17)

Thus, it completes the proof of the first assertion of Proposition
4.0.1
.

Next, the a priori estimation of
\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}
can be obtain with the help of (
4.17
). In order to do so, we consider the first integral equation in the system (
2.7
), and obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big)\Bigg[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Bigg](\mathrm{x},t)=\mathbb{H}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big](\mathrm{x},t)+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big](\mathrm{x},t)

\displaystyle\text{i.e.}\quad\quad\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}=\Bigg[\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Bigg]^{-1}\Bigg[\mathbb{H}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Bigg]+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Bigg[\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Bigg]^{-1}\Bigg[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big]\Bigg]

(4.18)

from which we derive

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\Big[\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big]^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R})\Big)}\Big\|\mathbb{H}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big\|\Big[\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big]^{-1}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}\Big(\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R)}\Big)}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}.

(4.19)

Then, we deduce from Lemma
3.7
, Lemma
3.13
, Corollary
3.5.1
i.e. the continuity of the operator
\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\xi}\overline{\mathcal{V}}:\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathrm{B}\times\mathbb{R})\to\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
, and observing the fact
|\mathrm{E}|^{2}
is independent of the time t in
(0,\mathrm{T}_{0})

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0})}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\xi}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{T}_{0}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.20)

Combining (
4.1
) and (
4.1
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{-1}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.21)

Consequently, after plugging (
4.17
) in the previous expression, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big(\alpha^{\frac{1}{4}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta+\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{\mathrm{p}}}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big)\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}.

(4.22)

Therefore, it completes the proof of second assertion of Proposition
4.0.1
.

4.2
Estimation of the Heat Potential’s Dominating Term

The heat propagation outside and close to the nanoparticle can be estimated using the following relation:

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{S}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{ext}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}\Big](\xi,t)+\mathcal{D}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{ext}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}\Big](\xi,t),\quad\quad\text{for}\ (\xi,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\overline{\Omega}\times(0,\mathrm{T}),

(4.23)

Rewriting the above expression explicitly with the transmission condition as (
1.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{ext}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau+\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{ext}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{ext}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle=-\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big]\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau.

Now, noticing the fact that, for fixed
(\xi,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\overline{\Omega}\times(0,\mathrm{T})
, the function
\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)
is sufficiently smooth with respect to
(\mathrm{y},\tau)\in\partial\Omega\times(0,t)
, it follows from Taylor’s series expansion for
\mathrm{z}\in\Omega
and by duality pairing that

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big]\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Big\|\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,t)\Big)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,t)\Big)}\Bigg).

Therefore, we deduce that

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(1)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Bigg).

Then, applying interpolation theory and observing that
\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)
are independent of space variables, i.e.
\xi
is outside
\overline{\Omega}
, we derive the following:

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Big\|\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,t)\Big)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,\mathrm{t})}\Big\|\nabla\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\cdot)\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}(0,\mathrm{t})}\Bigg).

(4.24)

Let us estimate the integral

\textbf{err}^{(2)}:=\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau.

As
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
, due to duality between the function space
\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t)}\Big)
, based on the standard Sobolev embedding
\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\mathrm{B}\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{i}}\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\mathrm{B}\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
, and using Lemma
3.7
we obtain the following estimate:

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\Bigg|\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha^{\frac{1}{4}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\mathrm{B}\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}}\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}.

Analogously, we deduce that

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(2)}:=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{y},\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Bigg).

(4.25)

Consequently, due to (
4.2
), (
4.25
), we derive the explicit form of
\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)\ \text{for}\ (\xi,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{T})

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t})=-\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau+\textbf{err}^{(1)}+\textbf{err}^{(2)}.

(4.26)

To derive an explicit form of the first term in the approximation of
\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)
, we start with the first integral equation of the system (
2.7
)

\displaystyle\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}I_{d}-\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Bigg)\Bigg[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Bigg]=\mathbb{H}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{x}}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big].

(4.27)

We multiply this integral equation with
\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)
and integrate with respect to the spatial boundary
\partial\Omega
to obtain the following:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}-\int_{\partial\Omega}\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\text{Lap}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\partial\Omega}\Big(\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}-\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\text{Lap}}\Big)\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}=\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\mathbb{H}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big](\mathrm{x},\mathrm{t})d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\mathcal{V}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big](\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}

(4.28)

where we denote by
\mathcal{K}^{*}_{\text{Lap}}
the spatial adjoint of the double layer operator of the Laplacian (i.e. the Neumann-Poincaré operator). Then, as
\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)
is a constant, in the the space variable ’y’, then, refer to
[
35
]
,
\mathcal{K}_{\textbf{Lap}}\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big](y)=-\frac{1}{2}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)
, i.e.
-\frac{1}{2}
is an eigenvalue of the Neumman-Poincaré operator. This allows us to rewrite the above equation after integrating in time domain as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}-\mathcal{K}_{\text{Lap}}\Big)\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big](\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{v}}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}d\mathrm{s}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau.

(4.29)

Now, we can represent the term
\Big(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}-\mathcal{K}_{\text{Lap}}\Big)\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big]
as

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}-\mathcal{K}_{\text{Lap}}\Big)\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big](\mathrm{y},\tau)=\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}}{2\pi|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\Big[\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}

(4.30)

where we set

\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},t,\tau):=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\dfrac{\alpha|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}{4(\mathrm{s}-\tau)^{2}}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\dfrac{\alpha|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}{4(\mathrm{s}-\tau)}\Big)\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};\mathrm{z},\mathrm{s})d\mathrm{s}.

(4.31)

Consequently, we derive

Expression

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}}{2\pi|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\Big[\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle+\Big\langle\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)},\mathbb{H}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})}.

(4.32)

In the following expression, we apply integration by parts, Leibniz’s rule for integration, change of variables and observe the fact that the Newtonian heat potential satisfies the corresponding in-homogeneous equation
(\alpha\partial_{\mathrm{t}}-\Delta)\mathcal{V}(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{t})=f(\mathrm{x},\mathrm{t})
to rewrite the leading term of (
4.2
) as follows:

First Term

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};\mathrm{z},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\int_{\Omega}\Delta\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\int_{\Omega}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\partial_{\tau}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle-\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\partial_{\tau}|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v}-\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{v},\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v}-\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle--\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle=-\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}d\tau-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{y}d\tau\Bigg].

(4.33)

Furthermore, we consider the second term of the previous expression
’First Term’
(
4.2
), i.e.

\displaystyle\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

(4.34)

and derive its estimate. To do that let us recall the initial heat potential for
|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\in\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big](\mathrm{y},\tau)=\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}.

Therefore, with the help of Lemma
3.6
, due to duality pairing between the spaces
H^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)
and
H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)
, and by Sobolev embedding
\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{i}}H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)
the following estimate holds

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(3)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big\|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Big\|\mathbb{I}_{0}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0})}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\cdot)\Big\|_{H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)}\Big\|\mathbb{I}_{0}\Big[|\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\Omega\times\mathbb{R_{+}}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}}\delta^{2}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\tilde{t};z,\cdot)\Big\|_{H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta\Big\|\mathbb{I}_{0}\Big[|\hat{\mathrm{E}}|^{2}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}}\delta^{2}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\tilde{t};z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(B\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\Big)}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta\Big\||\hat{\mathrm{E}}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(B\Big)}.

(4.35)

Observing that
|\hat{\mathrm{E}}|^{2}
is independent of the time
t
in
(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})
and
\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}
is independent on the space variable, we can rewrite the above expression as

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(3)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}}\delta^{2}\Big\|\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\tilde{t};z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta}\Big)}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\delta\Big\||\hat{\mathrm{E}}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(B\Big)}.

Consequently, when we scale back to
\Omega
, the following expression can be derived

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(3)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{y}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\delta\Big\|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(0,T_{0}\Big)}\alpha^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{p}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{T_{0}}\Big|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big|^{2}}d\tau\Bigg).

(4.36)

Thus, we deduce from (
4.2
) and (
4.2
) that

First Term

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1,\mathrm{y}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}\Phi(\mathrm{y},\tau;\mathrm{v},\mathrm{s})|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{v})d\mathrm{v}d\mathrm{s}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle=-\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}d\tau+\textbf{err}^{(3)}.

(4.37)

Next, we proceed to estimate the second term of (
4.2
) defined as:

\displaystyle\textbf{Second Term}:=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}}{2\pi|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\Big[\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau.

(4.38)

To do that, we define

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},t):=\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}}{2\pi|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\Big[\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}.

(4.39)

Then, based on the duality pairing between the spaces
\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}
and
\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}
, we obtain

Second Term

\displaystyle:=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}}{2\pi|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\Big[\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t})\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t})\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}.

(4.40)

We state the following lemma. As its justification is rather lengthy but elementary, we postpone it to the Appendix.

Lemma 4.1

We set
\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)
as follows:

\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau):=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\dfrac{\alpha|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}{4(\mathrm{s}-\tau)^{2}}\textbf{exp}\Big(-\dfrac{\alpha|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}{4(\mathrm{s}-\tau)}\Big)\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};\mathrm{z},\mathrm{s})d\mathrm{s}.

(4.41)

Then we have

\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};\mathrm{z},\tau)=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\sqrt{\alpha}|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|\ \|\partial_{\mathrm{s}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};\mathrm{z},\cdot)\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}(0,t)}\Bigg),

(4.42)

for
\mathrm{x},\mathrm{y}
such that
|\mathrm{v}-\mathrm{y}|<<1
and
\mathrm{t}\in(0,\mathrm{T}]
uniformly with respect to
\Omega
.

Recall that if
\Omega
is a bounded open subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
of class
\mathcal{C}^{1,\alpha}
, with
\alpha\in(0,1]
, then there exist a constant
c_{\Omega,\alpha}>0
such that

|(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}|\leq c_{\Omega,\alpha}|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{1+\alpha}.

Using Lemma
4.1
, the duality pairing on spatial domain, and the Sobolev embedding
\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\mathrm{B}\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{i}}\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\mathrm{B}\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{1}:=\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t})\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Bigg|\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}}{2\pi|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\Big[\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}\Bigg|^{2}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\mathrm{t}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{|(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}|^{2}}{|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}\Big\|\partial_{\mathrm{s}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{4}}(0,t)}d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{9}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Big\|\partial_{\mathrm{s}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{9}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T_{0}})}\partial_{\mathrm{s}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{9}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T_{0,\delta}})}\partial_{\tilde{\mathrm{s}}}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{9}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\alpha^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathrm{m}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\partial_{\tilde{\mathrm{s}}}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{9}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Big\|\partial_{\mathrm{s}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{T}_{0})\Big)}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{\frac{11}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\Big\|\partial_{\mathrm{s}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{T}_{0})\Big)}dt\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}\delta^{6}\int_{0}^{t}\Big\|\partial_{\mathrm{s}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(0,T_{0})}dt\Bigg).

(4.43)

The above expression needs to be estimated with respect to the
H^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
-norm.
As
\mathcal{K}_{\textbf{Lap}}\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big](y)=-\frac{1}{2}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)
, then

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{K}_{\text{Lap}}\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}=\frac{1}{4}\Big\|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}.

(4.44)

Also, from the continuity of the operator
\mathcal{K}:H^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\rightarrow H^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
, see
[
24
]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathcal{K}\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}\lesssim\Big\|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}.

(4.45)

We set
\mathbb{E}:=\Big(\mathcal{K}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}-\mathcal{K}_{\text{Lap}}\Big)\Big[\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big].

Hence, with the help of the previous two expression (
4.44
) and (
4.45
), considering the independence of
\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)
on space variable, and observing the fact that we are only concerned with the time interval
(0,\mathrm{T_{0}})
, we derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},t)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t)}\Big)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},t)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}\lesssim\Big\|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\cdot)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\Big[|\nabla_{\textbf{tan}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|^{2}+\Big|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big|^{2}+|\partial_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|^{2}\Big]d\tau\Bigg).

(4.46)

Thus, by interpolation, we obtain, based on (
4.2
) and (
4.2
)

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},t)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},t)\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{1,\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t)}\Big)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\Big\|\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{y},t)\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t)}\Big)}^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{\frac{7}{4}}\sqrt[4]{\mathbb{V}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{V}_{2}}\Bigg).

(4.47)

Consequently, we provide the following estimate with the help of previous estimate (
4.2
) and inserting it into (
4.2
)

Second Term

\displaystyle:=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{(\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v})\cdot\nu_{\mathrm{v}}}{2\pi|\mathrm{y}-\mathrm{v}|^{2}}\Big[\varphi(\mathrm{v},\mathrm{y},\mathrm{t},\tau)-\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big]d\sigma_{\mathrm{v}}d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{\frac{7}{4}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\sqrt[4]{\mathbb{V}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{V}_{2}}\Bigg).

Additionally, we redefine the term above with the following notation

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(4)}:=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\delta^{\frac{7}{4}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\sqrt[4]{\mathbb{V}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{V}_{2}}\Bigg).

(4.48)

As for the third term of (
4.2
), in the same way as elliptic equations, the bi-linear form of the hyper-singular heat operator can be represented as a weakly singular operator. Moreover, we state the following result, see
[
9
, Theorem 6.1]
for details.

Lemma 4.2

Let
\partial_{T}
and
\partial_{\mathrm{t}}
denote the derivative with respect to the arc length on
\Gamma
and the time derivative, respectively. let
\underline{\mathrm{n}}
denote the exterior normal vector. Then

\displaystyle\Big\langle\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)},\mathbb{H}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\partial_{\mathrm{T}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)},\mathcal{S}\Big[\partial_{\mathrm{T}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}}-\Big\langle\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)}\underline{\mathrm{n}},\partial_{t}\mathcal{S}\Big[\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}},

where
\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)}
and
\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\in\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big).

It was noted in
[
9
]
that we should use the following identity together with the distributional time derivative on
\mathbb{R}\times\Gamma
,
\Big\langle\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)}\underline{\mathrm{n}},\partial_{t}\mathcal{S}\Big[\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}}=\Big\langle\partial_{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)}\underline{\mathrm{n}},\mathcal{S}\Big[\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}}
. There is an alternative representation of the above formula given in
[
32
, Theorem 4.1]
.

If we set
\psi\displaystyle\equiv\Phi(\xi,t;z,\tau)
, then from Lemma
4.2
we deduce

Third Term

\displaystyle:=\Big\langle\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\mathrm{t};z,\mathrm{\tau)},\mathbb{H}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\rangle_{(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}}

\displaystyle=-\Big\langle\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{T}_{\mathrm{i}}\underline{\mathrm{n}}],\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{t}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\rangle_{(\partial\Omega)_{\mathrm{T}}}.

(4.49)

In the next step, we use Lemma
3.7
, Lemma
3.11
, duality of the function space
\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)
, Sobolev embeddings
\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{i}}H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
,
\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{i}}\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)
and continuity of the single layer operator
\overline{\mathcal{S}}:H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}{\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\to\mathrm{L}^{2}{\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}
, to obtain the following estimate:

Third Term

\displaystyle:=\Big\langle\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\underline{\mathrm{n}}],\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{t}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\rangle_{\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{t}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}\Big\|\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{t})\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathrm{t}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\Big\|\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{t}}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\tilde{t};z,\tilde{\tau})\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\|\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}}\Big[\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\hat{\mathrm{T}}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big]\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{t}}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\tilde{t};z,\tilde{\tau})\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\|\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\hat{\mathrm{U}}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{H^{-1,-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{t}}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\tilde{t};z,\tilde{\tau})\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\|\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\hat{\mathrm{U}}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\chi$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\chi$}}}_{(0,\mathrm{T}_{0,\delta})}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{t}}\hat{\Phi}^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,\tilde{t};z,\tilde{\tau})\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\alpha_{\mathrm{p}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\delta^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big\|\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\hat{\mathrm{U}}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial B\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\underline{\mathrm{n}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\partial\Omega\times(0,\mathrm{T}_{0})\Big)}\delta\Big\|\underline{\mathrm{n}}\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T}_{0}}\Big|\partial_{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big|^{2}d\tau}\Bigg).

(4.50)

To further simplify the above expression, we redefine it with the following notation

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(5)}:=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{\frac{3}{2}}\Big\|\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{0}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}\Big\|_{\mathrm{H}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\partial\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\Big)}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T}_{0}}\Big|\partial_{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big|^{2}d\tau}\Bigg).

(4.51)

Now, considering (
4.2
), (
4.48
) and (
4.51
) and inserting these into (
4.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau

\displaystyle=-\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}d\tau+\textbf{err}^{(3)}+\textbf{err}^{(4)}

\displaystyle+\textbf{err}^{(5)}.

(4.52)

We recall the previously derived expression

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\Bigg[-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\partial\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\gamma^{\textbf{int}}_{1}\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{i}}(\mathrm{y},\tau)d\sigma_{\mathrm{y}}d\tau+\textbf{err}^{(1)}+\textbf{err}^{(2)}\Bigg].

(4.53)

Therefore, using Proposition
4.0.1
, considering the regime
\frac{1}{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\sim\delta^{2}
,
\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}\sim 1
, inserting (
4.2
) in (
4.53
) we deduce the desired expression of the heat potential around the inserted plasmonic nanoparticle for
(\xi,t)\in(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\overline{\Omega})_{T}
and
\mathrm{z}\in\Omega
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\Bigg[\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}d\tau+\textbf{err}^{(1)}+\textbf{err}^{(2)}+\textbf{err}^{(3)}+\textbf{err}^{(4)}+\textbf{err}^{(5)}\Bigg],

(4.54)

where

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(1)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{7}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{\frac{7}{2}-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg),\ \textbf{err}^{(2)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{7}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-\cdot|^{2-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg)

\textbf{err}^{(3)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{4}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\Big|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big|^{2}}d\tau\Bigg),\ \textbf{err}^{(4)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{\frac{23}{4}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\sqrt[4]{\mathbb{V}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{V}_{2}}\Bigg)

and

\textbf{err}^{(5)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{6}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\Big|\partial_{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big|^{2}d\tau}\Bigg).

Next, we derive a simplified form of the above error terms. In order to do so, we compute the classical singularity estimate for the fundamental solution
\Phi(\xi,t;z,\tau)
and we also refer to
[
19
, Chapter 1]
,
[
26
, Chapter 9]
. The key is to use the inequality
\mathrm{s}^{\mathrm{r}}e^{-\mathrm{s}}\leq\mathrm{r}^{\mathrm{r}}e^{-\mathrm{m}}
with
0<\mathrm{s},\mathrm{r}<\infty
and
\mathrm{s}:=\alpha|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{2}/4(t-\tau)
. Thus we deduce the following singular estimates

\displaystyle\Big|\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{r}}}{(t-\tau)^{r}}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{2-2r}},\ \ \ \mathrm{r}<1,

(4.55)

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{\mathrm{x}_{i}}\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{r}}}{(t-\tau)^{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{3-2\mathrm{r}}},\ \ \ \mathrm{r}<2,\ i=1,2,

(4.56)

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{\nu}\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{\mathrm{r}}}{(t-\tau)^{r}}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{2-2r}},\ \ \ \mathrm{r}<2,\mbox{ for }x,y\in\partial\Omega,

(4.57)

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{t}\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{1-r}}{(t-\tau)^{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{4-2\mathrm{r}}},\ \ \ r<2,

(4.58)

and

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Phi(\mathrm{x},t;\mathrm{y},\tau)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{r}}{(t-\tau)^{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y}|^{3-2\mathrm{r}}},\ \ \ r<2.

(4.59)

From the above estimates we deduce that
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|^{2}d\tau\lesssim\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{4-4\mathrm{r}}}\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\frac{1}{(t-\tau)^{2\mathrm{r}}}d\tau
.
We denote by
\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}:=\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\frac{1}{(t-\tau)^{2\mathrm{r}}}dt\ \ <+\infty\ \ \text{for}\ \mathrm{r}<\frac{1}{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}|\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|^{2}d\tau}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{2-2\mathrm{r}}}\Bigg)

and therefore,

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(3)}:=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\varepsilon^{4}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{2-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg).

(4.60)

We also note that
\nabla_{\text{tan}}u:=\nabla u-(\partial_{\nu}u)\nu
. Analogously, we find

\displaystyle\sqrt[4]{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\Big[|\nabla_{\textbf{tan}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|^{2}+\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)^{2}+\Big(\partial_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)\Big)^{2}\Big]d\tau}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\sqrt[4]{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{\frac{3}{2}-\mathrm{r}}}\Bigg).

(4.61)

Based on the previous estimates, we also obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\sqrt[4]{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}|\partial_{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|^{2}d\tau dt}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\sqrt[4]{\mathcal{S}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{2-2\mathrm{r}}}\Bigg),

(4.62)

where we denote by
\mathcal{S}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}\equiv\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}\frac{1}{(t-\tau)^{2\mathrm{r}}}d\tau\ \ <+\infty\ \ \text{for}\ \mathrm{r}<1.

Thus we obtain from (
4.61
) and (
4.62
)
\sqrt[4]{\mathbb{V}_{1}\cdot\mathbb{V}_{2}}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\sqrt[4]{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}\mathcal{S}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{\frac{7}{2}-2\mathrm{r}}}\Bigg),

and then

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(4)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\varepsilon^{\frac{23}{4}}\sqrt[4]{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}\mathcal{S}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{\frac{7}{2}-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg).

(4.63)

Next, we further derive the estimate
\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\mathrm{T_{0}}}|\partial_{t}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|^{2}d\tau}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{4-2\mathrm{r}}}\Bigg).

Therefore, the following can be obtained

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(5)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\varepsilon^{6}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{4-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg).

(4.64)

Furthermore, with the similar analysis as above we obtain

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(1)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{7}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{\frac{7}{2}-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg)

(4.65)

and base on the estimate (
4.57
)

\displaystyle\textbf{err}^{(2)}=\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{7}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-\cdot|^{2-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg).

(4.66)

Hence, considering
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\overline{\Omega}
,
\mathrm{y}\in\partial\Omega
,
z\in\Omega
, inserting (
4.60
), (
4.63
), (
4.64
), (
4.65
) and (
4.66
) in (
4.54
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\Bigg[\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;z,\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}d\tau+\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{4}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{2-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{6}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{4-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{\frac{23}{4}}\sqrt[4]{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}\mathcal{S}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{\frac{7}{2}-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{7}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-z|^{\frac{7}{2}-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg)+\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\delta^{7}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\frac{1}{|\xi-\cdot|^{2-2\mathrm{r}}}\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\Bigg)\Bigg].

(4.67)

The a priori estimates of the electric fields
\mathrm{E}
are given in the next proposition:

Proposition 4.2.1

The electric field corresponding to either the plasmonic or dielectric nanoparticles enjoy the following a priori estimates:

\displaystyle\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta^{-1}\Big),

(4.68)

and

\displaystyle\Big\||\mathrm{E}|^{2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{L}^{2}\Big(\Omega\Big)}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\delta|\log\delta|^{\frac{3\mathrm{h}}{2}}\Big),

(4.69)

respectively.

Proof.
See Section
5.4
and Section
6
respectively.

Let us take
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\setminus{\overline{\Omega}}
such that
dist(\xi,\Omega)\sim\delta^{p}
and then
|\xi-z|\sim\delta^{p}+\delta
.

Using (
4.68
) in Proposition
4.2.1
, with the choice of
\mathrm{p}
and
\mathrm{r}<\frac{1}{2}
, such that
\frac{1+2\mathrm{p}(1-\mathrm{r})}{2}<\mathrm{h}<1
we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{\mathrm{e}}(\xi,t)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}{\gamma_{\mathrm{m}}}\frac{1}{\alpha_{\mathrm{m}}}\Bigg[\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi\gamma_{\mathrm{p}}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\Phi^{\textbf{e}}(\xi,t;\mathrm{z},\tau)|\mathrm{E}|^{2}(\mathrm{y})d\mathrm{y}d\tau+\mathcal{O}\Bigg(\frac{\omega\cdot\bm{\Im}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{p}})}{2\pi}\delta^{3-2\mathrm{p}(1-\mathrm{r})}\sqrt{\mathcal{K}^{(\mathrm{T_{0}})}_{\mathrm{r}}}\Bigg)\Bigg].

(4.70)

This completes the first part of the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Next, under the estimate (
4.69
) of Proposition
4.2.1
and with the condition
